# Rigveda Samhita — Volume 23 (Kannada → English translation)

## Preface (Pīṭhike)

Although many deities are praised in the Vedas, and especially in the Ṛgveda, the chief among them are Indra, Agni, the Viśvedevas, the Aśvins, Dyāvāpṛthivī, Uṣas, the Maruts, Mitra, Varuṇa and Āditya. Even among these deities, no other deity has the position that Indra has. The position that Śiva and Viṣṇu hold in the Purāṇas and the like, and the rank and standing that Brahman (Parabrahman) holds in Vedānta, belong to Indra in the Ṛgveda. Indra is praised in many ways, for his qualities and his deeds of valour, as one who protects the good and also punishes the wicked. When the seers praise Indra, they pray that he grant them, as the reward for their praise and for sacrifices and other good rites, food and the like, wealth, a house, heroic sons and protection from the wicked; and that he destroy the Asuras, who are wicked and who create obstacles to the performance of good rites.

There are many ancient accounts that, when destroying the Asuras, he kills those Asuras who fight with deceit, fraud and crooked means by fighting them with the very same kind of means. To give an example, the cleverness Indra used when he had to destroy the Asura named Namuci is a delight. We shall relate it here briefly. Namuci was a demon. Many Ṛks of the Ṛgveda indicate that he was a renowned chief among the early inhabitants of this land who are called Dāsas. Since he was extremely strong, it was very hard for Indra to fight and conquer him. On this subject the destruction of Namuci is indicated in the Ṛks Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-53-7; 2-14-5; 7-19-5; 10-23-7; 5-30-7; 5-30-8; 6-20-6; 8-14-13, and in addition the Taittirīya Brāhmaṇa (Kṛṣṇa Yajurveda) gives a somewhat fuller account of it. That is what we have written here.

इंद्रो वृत्रग्ं हत्वा । असुरान् पराभाव्य । नमुचिमासुरं नालभत । तग्ं शच्यागृह्णात् । तौ समलभेतां । सोऽस्मादभितुनतरोऽभवत् । सोऽब्रवीत् । संधाग्ं संदधावहै । अथ त्वावप्रक्ष्यामि । न मा शुष्केण नार्द्रॆण हनः । न दिवा न नक्तमिति । स एतमपां फेनमसिंचत् । न वा एष शुष्को नार्दो व्युष्टासीत् । अनुदितः सूर्यः । न वा एतद्दिवा न नक्तं । तस्यैतस्मिन् लोके । अपां फेनेन शिर उदवर्तयत् । तदेनमन्ववर्तत । मित्रद्रुगिति । स एतानपामार्गानजनयत् । तानजुहोत् । तैर्वै स रक्षाग् स्यपाहत । यदपामार्गहोमो भवति । रक्षसामपहत्यै ॥ (*iṃdro vṛtragṃ hatvā | asurān parābhāvya | namucimāsuraṃ nālabhata | tagṃ śacyāgṛhṇāt | tau samalabhetāṃ | so 'smādabhitunataro 'bhavat | so 'bravīt | saṃdhāgṃ saṃdadhāvahai | atha tvāvaprakṣyāmi | na mā śuṣkeṇa nārdrèṇa hanaḥ | na divā na naktamiti | sa etamapāṃ phenamasiṃcat | na vā eṣa śuṣko nārdo vyuṣṭāsīt | anuditaḥ sūryaḥ | na vā etaddivā na naktaṃ | tasyaitasmin loke | apāṃ phenena śira udavartayat | tadenamanvavartata | mitradrugiti | sa etānapāmārgānajanayat | tānajuhot | tairvai sa rakṣāg syapāhata | yadapāmārgahomo bhavati | rakṣasāmapahatyai ‖*) (Tai. Brā. 1-7-1-6, 7)

### Printed p. ii

Formerly, after Indra had killed the Asura Vṛtra, he also destroyed other demons. But he could not conquer the demon Namuci. This one was extremely powerful and yielded to no kind of weapon. So Indra thought that he must conquer him by his own bodily strength and began a wrestling-fight with him. Yet the more they fought, the more Namuci's strength grew. Indra could not escape his grip. Then Indra asked Namuci to release him, and the Asura addressed Indra: "O Indra, if I am to release you, you must do as I say. Let us now make a pact with each other. You may again wish to fight me by some stratagem and kill me. On such an occasion the weapon you use to kill me must be neither wet nor dry. You must not kill me by day, nor by night. If you agree to these conditions, I shall release you." Indra agreed, "So be it", freed himself from his grip, and began to think how he might conquer him.

After some time, when Indra was wandering on the seashore, a device occurred to his mind: the foam of the sea fell within his sight. This foam is neither wet nor dry. "Therefore I shall make this my weapon and destroy Namuci. When the night has passed and the dawn begins, before the sun rises it is fully bright. That time, since it is not dark, is not night, and since the sun has not risen, is not day. If I fight Namuci at such a time and kill him with a weapon made of water-foam, I shall not have broken my word." Having resolved thus, he fought Namuci accordingly and cut off his head with that foam. Even so the demon did not die, and came chasing Indra in order to kill him. "O Indra, betrayer of a friend, stop, stop! You gave your word that you would not kill me, and now you have done such a deed of betrayal of a friend. Even now I shall not leave you unkilled," he said. Then Indra, frightened, created a special tree called the apāmārga and, for the increase of his vigour, made an offering in the fire with its sticks. By that Indra's strength increased and he destroyed that Namuci. Therefore it is said that those who wish to escape the afflictions of demons should make offerings with apāmārga sticks. Such delightful ancient accounts and episodes are abundant in the Vedas. The same, with slight variations, are described at length in the Purāṇas and the like.

To publish such a Ṛgveda Saṃhitā — most ancient, most profound, most vast, and the foundation of all the śāstras — in simple Kannada, together with a critical translation, is no easy task. Nevertheless, with the weighty purpose that through such a great undertaking all people, from scholars to the unlearned, may gain growth of knowledge

### Printed p. iii

and become worthy of God's grace, His Highness Śrī Jayacāmarājendra Oḍeyar Bahadur, G.C.B., G.C.S.I., sovereign of the throne of Karnāṭaka, patron of Vedic learning, himself a scholar, beloved of his subjects and devoted to God, and his Government, are encouraging this publication, which demands great expense, with their generous patronage; this must be called the good fortune of the multitude of the people of Karnāṭaka. I pray that Sarveśvara, the Vedapuruṣa, grant to our generous-minded sovereign, his family, the princesses, the prince bearing the auspicious name Śrīkaṇṭhadatta Nṛsiṃharāja Oḍeyar, and all the people as well, all worldly fruits — long life, health, prosperity and every kind of wealth — and also spiritual knowledge of the highest kind, ever more abundantly.

### Printed p. 1

Om. Śrī 9.

Obeisance to the Great Lord of the Hosts (Gaṇādhipati).

I bow to the elephant-faced one, whom the gods led by Vāgīśa (the Lord of Speech) bow to at the start of every undertaking, and so become successful.

I revere Vidyātīrtha-Maheśvara, whose breath the Vedas are, and who made the whole world out of the Vedas.

The Veda is Śiva and Śiva is the Veda; one who studies the Veda is Sadāśiva. Therefore one should always, with every effort, recite the Veda alone.

Ṛgveda Saṃhitā with Sāyaṇa's Bhāṣya, Part 23.

Fifth Aṣṭaka, Sixth Adhyāya.

Chapters 6–8 of the fifth Aṣṭaka. Chapter 1 of the sixth Aṣṭaka. Sūktas 81–104 of the seventh Maṇḍala. Sūktas 1–20 of the eighth Maṇḍala.

Of the sūktas seen by the seer Vasiṣṭha in the seventh Maṇḍala, the seven sūktas 75–81 have Uṣas as deity. Of these, the six sūktas 75–80 have already been explained in the previous part. This chapter begins with the last sūkta, 81. Since the description of the goddess Uṣas has been set out at length in Ṛ. Saṃ. Part 5, pp. 560–563, and elsewhere, no special explanation is needed here. Therefore only the word-meanings and the purport of the Ṛks, together with the explanation of difficult words, are given.

### Printed p. 2
## Sūkta 81 (Maṇḍala 7)

Sūkta 81 of the seventh Maṇḍala.

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अथ षष्ठो व्याख्यायते । सप्तमे मंडले पंचमेऽनुवाके दश सूक्तानि
> व्याकृतानि । प्रतु, आदर्शीति षडृचमेकादशं सूक्तं वसिष्ठस्यार्षमुषस्यं । प्रथमाद्या अयुजो बृहत्यो द्वितीयाद्या युजः सतोबृहत्यः । तथा चानुक्रांतं ।
> प्रत्यु षट् प्रागाधमिति ॥ प्रातरनुवाक उषस्यॆ क्रत्ौ बार्हते छंदस्याश्विनशस्त्रे
> चेदं सूक्तं । सूत्रितं च । प्रत्यु अदर्शि सह वामेनेति बार्हतं । आ. ४-१४ । इति॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says that the sixth (chapter) is now explained; in the fifth anuvāka of the seventh Maṇḍala ten sūktas have been explained. The eleventh sūkta, of six Ṛks beginning प्रतु, आदर्शीति (*pratu, ādarśīti*), has Vasiṣṭha as seer and Uṣas as deity. The first and the other odd Ṛks are Bṛhatī, the second and the other even Ṛks are Satobṛhatī. He cites the Anukramaṇī: प्रत्यु षट् प्रागाधमिति (*pratyu ṣaṭ prāgādhamiti*). The sūkta is used in the Prātaranuvāka, in the Uṣasya (Uṣas) rite in the Bārhata metre, and in the Āśvina-śastra. The sūtra says: प्रत्यु अदर्शि सह वामेनेति बार्हतं (*pratyu adarśi saha vāmeneti bārhataṃ*) (Ā. 4-14).

Anuvāda: Five chapters of the fifth Aṣṭaka are completed so far, so we now begin the explanation of the sixth chapter. Since the ten sūktas of the fifth anuvāka of the seventh Maṇḍala were explained in the previous chapter, we now begin the commentary on the eleventh sūkta. This sūkta, beginning "Praty u ādarśi", is the eleventh sūkta in the fifth anuvāka of the seventh Maṇḍala. It has six Ṛks. Vasiṣṭha is its seer; the goddess Uṣas is its deity. Its odd-numbered Ṛks, that is Ṛks 1, 3, 5, are in Bṛhatī metre. Its even-numbered Ṛks, that is Ṛks 2, 6, 6, are in Satobṛhatī metre. **NOTE** In the Anukramaṇī it is said "Praty u ṣaṭ prāgāthaṃ". It is explained by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra, "Praty u ādarśi saha vāmeneti bārhataṃ" (Ā. 4-14), that this sūkta is used, at the time of reciting the Prātaranuvāka mantras, for the Bṛhatī-metre Ṛks connected with the Upasya rite and for the Āśvina-śastra mantras.

Sūkta 81. Maṇḍala 7; Anuvāka 5; Sūkta 81. Aṣṭaka 5; Adhyāya 6; Varga 1. Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 6. Seer: Vasiṣṭha. Deity: Uṣas. Metre: 1, 3, 5 Bṛhatī; 2, 4, 6 Satobṛhatī.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्रत्यु॑ अदर्श्याय॒त्यु१॒॑गच्छंती॑ दुहि॒ता दि॒वः ।  
> अपो॒ महि॑ व्ययति॒ चक्ष॑से॒ तमो॒ ज्योति॑ष्कृणोति सू॒नरी॑ ॥१॥

### Printed p. 3

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्रति । ऊं इति । आदर्शि । आयती । उच्छंती । दुहिता । दिवः ।  
> अप्रो इति । महि । व्ययति । चक्षसे । तमः । ज्योतिः । कृणोति । सूनरी ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> आयत्यागच्छंती व्युच्छंती तमांसि निवासयंती वर्जयंती दिवो द्युलोकस्य सूर्यस्य वा दुहिता पुत्री एवंभूतोषाः प्रत्यदर्शि । सर्वैः प्रतिदृश्यते । उ
> इति पूरकः । सैषा महि महत्तमो नैशमंधकारं । अप उ इति निपातद्वयसमुदायोऽपेत्यस्यार्थे । अपो व्ययति । अपवृणोति । किमर्थं । चक्षसे दर्शनार्थं ।
> एवं कृत्वा सूनरी जनानां सुष्टु नेत्रुषा ज्योतिः प्रकाशं कृणोति । करोति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Uṣas, the daughter of heaven (or of the Sun), is seen by all as she comes, dawning, driving away the darknesses. उ (*u*) is a filler. She, the greatest, removes the darkness of night (अप उ (*apa u*) is a pair of particles in the sense of "away"; अपो व्ययति (*apo vyayati*) means "uncovers"), for the sake of sight. Having done so, she, the good leader of people, makes light.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- उच्छंती (*ucchaṃtī*)—destroying the darkness
- आयती (*āyatī*)—and coming
- दिवः (*divaḥ*)—of the heavenly world
- दुहिता (*duhitā*)—the daughter, the goddess Uṣas
- प्रति उ अदर्शि (*prati u adarśi*)—is visible to all
- चक्षसे (*cakṣase*)—for the sake of sight
- महि (*mahi*)—great
- तमः (*tamaḥ*)—darkness
- अपो व्ययति (*apo vyayati*)—she destroys
- सूनरी (*sūnarī*)—Uṣas, the excellent guide of people
- ज्योतिः (*jyotiḥ*)—light
- कृणोति (*kṛṇoti*)—she produces

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The goddess Uṣas, who comes destroying the darkness, the daughter of the heavenly world, is visible to all. For the sake of sight she destroys the great darkness. Uṣas, the excellent guide of people, produces light.

##### English translation
The daughter of heaven is everywhere beheld advancing and shedding light: she drives away the deep darkness that objects may meet the eye: the kind guide of man, she diffuses light.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

उ (*u*)—Here the particle उ (*u*) is used to fill out the pāda. This word has no specific meaning.

### Printed p. 4

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उदु॒स्रियाः॑ सृजते॒ सूर्यः॒ सचा॑ उ॒द्यन्नक्ष॑त्रमर्चि॒वत् ।  
> तवेदु॑षो॒ व्युषि॒ सूर्य॑स्य च॒ सं भ॒क्तेन॑ गमेमहि ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उत् । उस्रियाः । सृजते । सूर्यः । सचा । उत्यत् । नक्षत्रं । अर्चिवत् ।  
> तव । इत् । उषः । विउषि । सूर्यस्य । च । सं । भक्तेन । गमेमहि ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> सूर्यः सर्वस्य प्रेरक आदित्य उस्रिया रश्मिन् सचा सह युगपदेवोत्सृजते । उद्गमयति । तथोद्यदुद्धच्छत् प्रादुर्भवन्नक्षत्रं नभसि दृश्यमानं ग्रहनक्षत्रादिकमर्चिवद्दीप्तिमत्करोति । सौरेण तेजसा हि नक्तं चंद्रप्रभृतीनि नक्षत्राणि
> संते । सुषुम्नाः सूर्यरश्मिश्चंद्रमा गंधर्वः । वा. सं. १८-४८ । इति हि निगमः ।
> एवं च सति हे उष उषोदेवते तव सूर्यस्य च व्युषि विवासने प्रकाशने सति
> भक्तेनान्नेन सं गमेमहि । वयं संगच्छेमहि । इच्छब्दः पूरकः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The Sun, the impeller of all, sends forth his rays at the same moment; and rising, he makes the planets and stars seen in the sky shine. For by the Sun's brilliance the moon and the other stars shine at night. The Nigama (Vā. Saṃ. 18-48) says: सुषुम्नाः सूर्यरश्मिश्चंद्रमा गंधर्वः (*suṣumnāḥ sūryaraśmiścaṃdramā gaṃdharvaḥ*). This being so, O Uṣas, when you and the Sun dawn forth, may we be united with food (भक्तेन (*bhaktena*)). The word इत् (*it*) is a filler.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सूर्यः (*sūryaḥ*)—the Sun-god, the impeller of all
- उस्रियाः (*usriyāḥ*)—his rays
- सचा (*sacā*)—together with Uṣas
- उत् सृजते (*ut sṛjate*)—he makes them shine and spreads them
- उद्यत् (*udyat*)—rising
- नक्षत्रं (*nakṣatraṃ*)—the stars and the rest
- अर्चिवत् (*arcivat*)—he makes them radiant
- उषः (*uṣaḥ*)—O goddess Uṣas
- तव इत् (*tava it*)—of you, and
- सूरस्य च (*sūrasya ca*)—of the Sun
- व्युषि (*vyuṣi*)—when the simultaneous manifestation takes place
- भक्तेन (*bhaktena*)—with food
- सं गमेमहि (*saṃ gamemahi*)—may we be united

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The Sun-god, the impeller of all, makes his rays shine and spreads them together with Uṣas. As soon as he rises he makes the stars and the rest radiant. O goddess Uṣas, when you and the Sun manifest at the same time, may we be united with food; that is, may we obtain food.

### Printed p. 5

##### English translation
At the same time the sun sends forth his rays, and, rising, renders the planets luminous: so, Ushas, upon your manifestation, and that of Surya, may we become possessed of sustenance.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्रति॑ त्वा दुहितर्दिव॒ उषो॑ जी॒रा अ॑भुत्सहि ॥  
> या वह॑सि पु॒रु स्पा॒र्हं व॑नन्वति॒ रत्नं॒ न दा॒शुषे॒ मयः॑ ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्रति । त्वा । दुहितः । दिवः । उषः । जीराः । अभुत्सहि ।  
> या । वहसि । पुरु । स्वाह्यं । वनन्स्‌वति । रत्नं । न । दाशुषे । मयः ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे दिवो दुहितर्दिवोऽंतरिक्षाज्जायमाने हे उष उषोदेवते त्वा त्वां
> जीराः क्षिप्रकारिणो वयं प्रत्यभुत्स्महि । प्रतिबोद्धारो भवेम । हे वनन्वति ।
> वननं संभजनं संभक्तव्यं धनं वा । तद्वति या त्वं पुरु बहु स्पार्हं स्पृहणीयं
> धनं वहसि प्रापयसि दाशुषे हवींषि दत्तवते यजमानाय रत्नं रमणीयं
> धनमिव मयः सुखं च या त्वं वहसि तां त्वां प्रत्यभुत्स्महीत्यन्वयः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O daughter of heaven, born from the sky, O Uṣas, may we, the prompt ones (जीराः (*jīrāḥ*)), become awakeners of you. वनन्वति (*vananvati*): वननं (*vananaṃ*) is sharing, or wealth to be shared; she who possesses it. You who bring much desirable wealth, and delight, like a precious treasure, to the sacrificer who has given oblations — you we would awaken. This is the construction.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- दिवः दुहितः (*divaḥ duhitaḥ*)—O daughter of the sky
- वनन्वति (*vananvati*)—possessing wealth that serves for enjoyment
- उषः (*uṣaḥ*)—O goddess Uṣas
- या (*yā*)—you who
- पुरु (*puru*)—very abundant
- स्पार्हं (*spārhaṃ*)—much desired wealth you bring
- दाशुषे (*dāśuṣe*)—to the sacrificer who offers oblations
- रत्नं न (*ratnaṃ na*)—like delightful wealth
- मयः (*mayaḥ*)—you also provide happiness
- त्वा (*tvā*)—such as you
- जीराः (*jīrāḥ*)—we, the prompt ones
- प्रति अभ्युत्स्महि (*prati abhyutsmahi*)—may we have the power to awaken (you)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O goddess Uṣas, daughter of the sky, possessor of wealth that serves for enjoyment, you who bring very abundant and much-desired wealth, and who provide happiness, like delightful wealth, to the sacrificer who offers oblations — grant that we, the prompt ones, may have the power to awaken such a one as you.

### Printed p. 6

##### English translation
May we, prompt adorers, awaken you, Ushas, daughter of heaven bountiful divinity, who bring ample and desirable (wealth) and happiness, like riches, to the donor (of the oblation).

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उ॒च्छंती॒ या कृ॒णोषि॑ मं॒हना॑ महि प्र॒ख्यै दे॑वि॒ स्व॑र्दृ॒शे ॥  
> तस्या॑स्ते रत्न॒भाज॑ ईमहे व॒यं स्याम॑ मा॒तुर्न सू॒नवः॑ ॥४॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उच्छंती । या । कृणोषि । मंहना । महि । प्रख्यै । देवि । स्वः । दृशे ।  
> तस्याः । ते । रत्नभाजः । ईमहे । वयं । स्याम । मातुः । न । सूनवः ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे महि महति देवि दानादिगुणयुक्त उषोदेवते व्युच्छंती तमांसि
> वर्जयंती मंहना महिम्मा युक्ता । यद्वा । मंहतिर्दानकर्मा । दानयुक्ता ।
> या त्वं स्वः सर्वं जगत् प्रख्यॆ प्रबोधनार्थं दृशे दर्शनार्थं च कृणोषि
> करोषि तस्यास्तादृश्या रत्नभाजो रत्नानां रमणीयानां धनानां भाजयित्रा :
> सेवॆयित्रा ईमहे। याचामहे । किं। रत्नभाज इति समभिव्याहाराद्रत्नानीति गम्यते।
> अपि च वयं तव प्रियतमाः स्याम । भवेम । मातुर्न सूनवः । यथा मातुर्जनन्याः सूनवः पुत्राः प्रियतमा भवंति तद्वत् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O great goddess Uṣas, endowed with qualities such as bounty, who dawn, driving away the darkness, endowed with greatness (मंहना (*maṃhanā*)); or else, मंहति (*maṃhati*) is a root meaning giving, so "endowed with giving" — you who make the whole world (स्वः (*svaḥ*)) able for awakening (प्रख्यै (*prakhyai*)) and for seeing (दृशे (*dṛśe*)): to such a one, the distributor (रत्नभाजः (*ratnabhājaḥ*)) of delightful riches, we ask. What do we ask? From the context of रत्नभाज (*ratnabhāja*) it is understood: riches. And may we be most dear to you, as sons are most dear to a mother, their begetter.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- महि (*mahi*)—O great one
- देवि (*devi*)—O goddess Uṣas
- मंहना (*maṃhanā*)—endowed with greatness
- उच्छंती (*ucchaṃtī*)—destroying the darkness
- या (*yā*)—you who
- स्वः (*svaḥ*)—the whole world
- प्रख्यै (*prakhyai*)—for awakening
- दृशे (*dṛśe*)—for seeing
- कृणोषि (*kṛṇoṣi*)—you make capable **NOTE**
- तस्याः (*tasyāḥ*)—such a one
- रत्नभाजः (*ratnabhājaḥ*)—possessing delightful wealth
- ते (*te*)—of you
- सूनवः (*sūnavaḥ*)—children
- मातुः न (*mātuḥ na*)—as they are dear to their mothers
- वयं (*vayaṃ*)—we
- स्याम (*syāma*)—may we be dear
- ईमहे (*īmahe*)—we ask

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O great goddess Uṣas, you who are endowed with greatness and destroy the darkness, and who make the whole world capable of awakening and of seeing — to you, possessor of delightful wealth, we pray that we may be dear to you as children are dear to their mothers.

##### English translation
Mighty goddess, who, scattering darkness, are endowed with power to arouse the world and make it visible, we pray that we may be dear to you, the distributress of wealth, as sons are to a mother.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> तच्चि॒त्रं राध॒ आ भ॒रोषो॒ यद्दी॑र्घ॒श्रुत्त॑मं ।  
> यत्ते॑ दिवो दुहितर्मर्त॒भोज॑नं॒ तद्रा॑स्व भु॒नजा॑महै ॥५॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> तत् । चित्रं । राधः । आ । भर । उषः । यत् । दीर्घश्रुत् तमं ।  
> यत् । ते । दिवः । दुहितः । मर्तभोजनं । तत् । रास्व । भुनजामहै ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे उषः तच्चित्रं चायनीयं राधो धनमा भर । आहर । अस्मभ्यं प्रयच्छ ।
> युद्धनं दीर्घश्रुुत्तमं देशकालयोर्विप्रकर्षेऽप्यतिशयेन श्रोतव्यं । अपि च हे
> दिवो दुहितः ते तव त्वया देयं मर्तभोजनं मर्तानां मनुष्याणां भोगयोग्यं यदन्नमस्ति तद्रास्व । देह्यस्मभ्यं । वयं च त्वद्दत्तं धनमन्नं च भुनजामहै । अभ्यवहरेम ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Uṣas, bring (आ भर (*ā bhara*)) that wonderful (चित्रं (*citraṃ*)), desirable wealth (राधः (*rādhaḥ*)); give it to us — the wealth which is most renowned (दीर्घश्रुत्तमं (*dīrghaśruttamaṃ*)), to be heard of in the highest degree even across distance of place and time. And, O daughter of heaven, whatever food of yours, to be given by you, is fit for the enjoyment of mortals (humans), give (रास्व (*rāsva*)) that to us; and may we enjoy (भुनजामहै (*bhunajāmahai*)) the wealth and food given by you.

### Printed p. 8
#### Ṛk 5 (continued)

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- उषः (*uṣaḥ*)—O Uṣas, goddess of dawn
- यत् (*yat*)—which wealth
- दीर्घश्रुतमं (*dīrghaśrutamaṃ*)—is renowned for a very long time
- तत् (*tat*)—such
- चित्रं (*citraṃ*)—and attractive
- राधः (*rādhaḥ*)—wealth
- आभर (*ābhara*)—bring
- दिवः (*divaḥ*)—to the heavenly world
- दुहितः (*duhitaḥ*)—O Uṣas, daughter
- ते (*te*)—by you
- मर्त भोजनं (*marta bhojanaṃ*)—such as may be given, and fit for the enjoyment of humans
- यत् (*yat*)—whatever food there is
- तत् (*tat*)—that food
- रास्व (*rāsva*)—give to us
- भुनजामहै (*bhunajāmahai*)—may we enjoy it

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O goddess Uṣas, bring wealth that is renowned for a very long time, such wealth as is also attractive. O Uṣas, daughter of the heavenly world, give us whatever food there is that may be given by you and is fit for the enjoyment of humans. May we enjoy it.

##### English translation
Bring Ushas, such wondrous wealth as may be long renowned
bestow, daughter of heaven, that sustenance which is fit for mortals, such
as we may enjoy.
#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> श्रवः॑ सू॒रिभ्यो॑ अ॒मृतं॑ वसुत्व॒नं वाजा॑ अ॒स्मभ्यं॒ गोम॑तः ।  
> चो॒द॒यि॒त्रि म॒घोनः॑ सू॒नृता॑वत्यु॒षा उ॑च्छ॒दप॒ स्रिधः॑ ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> श्रवः । सूरिभ्यः । अमृतं । वसुत्वनं । वाजान् । अस्मभ्यं । गोमतः ।  
> चोदयित्रि । मघोनः । सूनृतावती । उषाः । उच्छत् । अप । स्रिधः ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे उषः सूरिभ्यः स्तोतृभ्योऽस्मभ्यममृतं मरणरहितं नित्यं वसुत्वनु
> वासकं वसुत्वयुक्तं वा श्रवः श्रवणीयं यशो रास्वत्यनुषंगः । तथा गोमत्तो

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Uṣas, give to us, the praisers (सूरिभ्यः (*sūribhyaḥ*) means "to the praisers"), fame (श्रवः (*śravaḥ*), that which is to be heard) that is immortal, that is, deathless and eternal, and that is wealth-bringing (वसुत्वनु (*vasutvanu*)) or wealth-endowed. The verb रास्व (*rāsva*) is carried on from the previous verse. Likewise give us foods (वाजा (*vājā*)) joined with many cows. The remaining persons are in the third person.
### Printed p. 9

> बहुभिर्गोभिर्युक्तान्नाजानन्नानि चास्मभ्यं रास्व । शिष्टः परोक्षकृतः । मघोनो
> हविष्मतो यजमानस्य चोदयित्रि प्रेरयित्रि सूनृतावती । प्रियसत्यात्मिका
> वाक्सूनृता । तद्वत्रुषाः स्त्रिधः शोषकाञ्छत्रूनपोचच्छत् । अपगमयतु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa continues the gloss. The rest of the verse is spoken in the third person, as an indirect address. मघोनो (*maghono*) is the sacrificer who has the oblation. चोदयित्रि (*codayitri*) is she who impels, and सूनृतावती (*sūnṛtāvatī*) is she who has सूनृता (*sūnṛtā*), speech that is by nature pleasant and true. May Uṣas, endowed with such speech, drive away the enemies called स्रिधः (*sridhaḥ*), that is, those who parch or wither, by the verb *apocacchat* (अपगमयतु (*apagamayatu*), "may she make them go away").

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सूरिभ्यः (*sūribhyaḥ*)—to us, who are praisers (O goddess Uṣas)
- अमृतं (*amṛtaṃ*)—that which is eternal
- वसुत्वनं (*vasutvanaṃ*)—and that which is endowed with wealth
- श्रवः (*śravaḥ*)—give fame
- गोमतः (*gomataḥ*)—accompanied by cows
- वाजान् (*vājān*)—foods
- अस्मभ्यं (*asmabhyaṃ*)—give to us
- मघोनः (*maghonaḥ*)—to the sacrificer who has the oblation
- चोदयित्री (*codayitrī*)—she who is the impeller
- सूनृतावती (*sūnṛtāvatī*)—and an excellent guide
- उषाः (*uṣāḥ*)—the goddess Uṣas
- स्रिधः (*sridhaḥ*)—the enemies who do harm
- अप उच्छत् (*apa ucchat*)—may she drive far away

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O goddess Uṣas, give us, the praisers, fame that is eternal and endowed with wealth. Give us food accompanied by cows. May the goddess Uṣas, the impeller of the sacrificer who has the oblation and his excellent guide, drive far away the enemies who do harm.

##### English translation
Grant to the pious immortal fame, conjoined with affluence: grant
to us food and cattle: and may Ushas, the encourager of the wealthy
(sacrificer), the speaker of truth, drive away our foes.
## Sūkta 82 (Maṇḍala 7)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इंद्रावरुणेति दशर्चं द्वादशं सूक्तं वसिष्ठस्यार्षं जागतं । इदमादीनि
> चत्वारि सूक्तानींद्रावरुणदेवतानि । तथा चानुक्रांतं । इंद्रावरुणा दश्येंद्रावरुणं ह जागतं त्विति ॥ तृतीये सवन उक्थ्ये प्रशास्तुः शस्त्र एतत्तूक्तं । सूत्रितं च।
> इंद्रावरुणा युवमा नां राजान् । आ ६-१ । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The twelfth sūkta, of ten Ṛks, beginning इंद्रावरुणेति (*iṃdrāvaruṇeti*), belongs to Vasiṣṭha as seer and is in the Jagatī metre. This sūkta and the three that follow it have Indra and Varuṇa as deity. So the Anukramaṇī says: इंद्रावरुणा दश्येंद्रावरुणं ह जागतं त्विति (*iṃdrāvaruṇā daśyeṃdrāvaruṇaṃ ha jāgataṃ tviti*). This sūkta is used in the Praśāstṛ's śastra at the third savana of the Ukthya rite, and the sūtra confirms it: इंद्रावरुणा युवमा नां राजान् (*iṃdrāvaruṇā yuvamā nāṃ rājān*) (Ā. 6-1).

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta beginning "इंद्रावरुणा (*iṃdrāvaruṇā*)" is the twelfth sūkta in the fifth anuvāka. It has ten Ṛks. Vasiṣṭha is the seer of this sūkta, Indra and Varuṇa are the deities, and the metre is Jagatī. The four sūktas from this one onward have Indra and Varuṇa as deity. The Anukramaṇī says: इंद्रावरुणा दश्येंद्रावरुणं ह जागतं (*iṃdrāvaruṇā daśyeṃdrāvaruṇaṃ ha jāgataṃ*). The use of this sūkta is for the śastra mantras recited by the ṛtvij called the Praśāstṛ at the time of the savana of the sacrifice named Ukthya, and this is explained in Āśvalāyana's Śrautasūtra by the sūtra "इंद्रावरुणा युवमा नां राजान् (*iṃdrāvaruṇā yuvamā nāṃ rājān*)" (Ā. 6-1). **NOTE**

##### Particulars

Maṇḍala 7. Anuvāka 5. Sūkta 82. Aṣṭaka 5. Adhyāya 6. Varga 2, 3. Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 10. Ṛṣi: Vasiṣṭha. Deity: Indra and Varuṇa. Metre: Jagatī.
#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इंद्रा॑ वरुणा यु॒वम॑ध्व॒राय॑ नो वि॒शे जना॑य॒ महि॒ ॑ 
> शर्म यच तं ।  
> दी॒र्घप्र॑यज्यु॒मति॒ यो व॑नु॒ष्यति॑ व॒यं ज॑येम॒ पृत॑नासु  ॒॑
> दूढ्यः ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इंद्रावरुणा। युवं । अध्वराय । नः । विशे । जनाय । महि । शर्म । यच्छतं ।  
> दीर्घप्रयज्युं । अति । यः । वनुष्यति वयं।जयेम । पृतनासु । दुःध्यः ॥१॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्रावरुणेंद्रावरुणौ युवं युवां नोऽस्माकं विशे निवेशयित्रे
> परिचारकाय जनाय पुत्रपौत्रादिलक्षणायाध्वराय यज्ञानुष्ठानार्थं महि महच्छर्म गृहं सुखं वा यच्छतं । प्रयच्छतं । अपि च दीर्घप्रयज्युं दीर्घप्रततयज्ञमस्मदीयं जनं यः शत्रुरति वनुष्यति अतिजिघांसति पृतनासु संग्रामेषु
> दूढ्यो दुर्धियो दुष्टाभिसंधींस्ताङ्छत्रून्वयं जयेम । अभिभवेम ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra and Varuṇa, grant to our servant person (विशे जनाय (*viśe janāya*), one who settles us in comfort), that is, sons, grandsons and the like, for the sacrifice (अध्वराय (*adhvarāya*)), for the performance of the sacrificial rite, a great शर्म (*śarma*), that is, a house or happiness (यच्छतं (*yacchataṃ*), "give"). Moreover, whatever enemy overly desires to kill (अति वनुष्यति (*ati vanuṣyati*)) our man whose sacrifice is long extended (दीर्घप्रयज्युं (*dīrghaprayajyuṃ*)), may we conquer in battles (पृतनासु (*pṛtanāsu*)) such enemies, who are दूढ्यः (*dūḍhyaḥ*), of evil mind and hostile design (जयेम (*jayema*), "may we overpower").
### Printed p. 12

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्रावरुणा (*iṃdrāvaruṇā*)—O Indra and Varuṇa
- युवं (*yuvaṃ*)—you
- नः (*naḥ*)—for our sake
- विशे (*viśe*)—to the man who worships
- जनाय (*janāya*)—to the person
- अध्वराय (*adhvarāya*)—so as to be favourable to the sacrifice
- महि (*mahi*)—great
- शर्म (*śarma*)—a house
- यच्छतं (*yacchataṃ*)—give
- दीर्घप्रयज्युं (*dīrghaprayajyuṃ*)—our man who is engaged in sacrifice for a long time
- यः (*yaḥ*)—whichever enemy
- अति वनुष्यति (*ati vanuṣyati*)—harms excessively
- पृतनासु (*pṛtanāsu*)—in battles
- दूड्यः (*dūḍyaḥ*)—such enemies who show an evil mind
- वयं (*vayaṃ*)—we
- जयेम (*jayema*)—may we conquer

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra and Varuṇa, give a great house, favourable to the sacrifice, to the man who worships for our sake. May we conquer in battles those enemies who show an evil mind, whichever enemy harms excessively our man who has long been engaged in sacrifice.

##### English translation
Indra and Varuna, bestow upon this man, our employer, a spacious chamber for (the celebration of) the sacrifice; and may we subdue in conflicts such evil-minded persons as may seek to injure him who has been long engaged (in your adoration).

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

वनुष्यति (*vanuṣyati*)—In giving the nirvacana of this word, Yāska takes the present Ṛk itself as the example and states the nirvacana for the second half of the Ṛk.
वनुष्यति । वनुष्यतिर्हंतिकर्मानवगतसंस्कारो भवति । वनुयाम वनुष्यतः इत्यपि निगमो भवति । दीर्घप्रयुज्युमति यो वनुष्यति वयं जयेम पृतनासु दूढ्यः । दीर्घप्रततयज्ञमभिजिघांसति यो वयं तं जयेम पृतनासु दूढ्यं दुर्धियं पापधियं । पापः पातापेयानां पापत्यमानोऽवाजीव पततीति वा पापत्यतेर्वा स्यात्॥ (*vanuṣyati | vanuṣyatirhaṃtikarmānavagatasaṃskāro bhavati | vanuyāma vanuṣyataḥ ityapi nigamo bhavati | dīrghaprayujyumati yo vanuṣyati vayaṃ jayema pṛtanāsu dūḍhyaḥ | dīrghapratatayajñamabhijighāṃsati yo vayaṃ taṃ jayema pṛtanāsu dūḍhyaṃ durdhiyaṃ pāpadhiyaṃ | pāpaḥ pātāpeyānāṃ pāpatyamāno 'vājīva patatīti vā pāpatyatervā syāt‖*) (Ni. 5-2)
The word वनुष्यति (*vanuṣyati*) means to harm, to kill. How this word is formed cannot be understood in accordance with grammar. "वनुयाम वनुष्यतः (*vanuyāma vanuṣyataḥ*)" is an example from a śruti sentence; that is, this word is stated in the Ṛks
त्वया वयं मघवन् पूर्वे धन इंद्रत्वोताः सासह्याम पृतन्यतो वनुयाम वनुष्यतः । (*tvayā vayaṃ maghavan pūrve dhana iṃdratvotāḥ sāsahyāma pṛtanyato vanuyāma vanuṣyataḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-132-1)
अस्माकेभिर्नृभिर्वयं सासह्याम पृतन्यतो वनुयाम वनुष्यतो नभंतामन्यके समे । (*asmākebhirnṛbhirvayaṃ sāsahyāma pṛtanyato vanuyāma vanuṣyato nabhaṃtāmanyake same |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-40-7)
The meaning of this sentence is: may we kill those who wish to harm us. As for the sentence in the present Ṛk,
दीर्घप्रयुज्युमति यो वनुष्यति वयं जयेम पृतनासु दूढ्यः (*dīrghaprayujyumati yo vanuṣyati vayaṃ jayema pṛtanāsu dūḍhyaḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-82-1)
the meaning is: may we conquer in battle the man who wishes to harm us, who are engaged in sacrifice for a long time. For the word दीर्घप्रयुज्युं (*dīrghaprayujyuṃ*), the gloss दीर्घप्रततयज्ञं (*dīrghapratatayajñaṃ*) means one engaged in a sacrifice that goes on for a long time, extended. May we conquer in battle the man of such evil mind, of sinful mind, who desires to harm us such as we are. दूढ्यं (*dūḍhyaṃ*) means दुर्धियं पापदियं (*durdhiyaṃ pāpadiyaṃ*), one of evil mind, one given to sinful deeds. As for पापः (*pāpaḥ*): because he drinks beverages not fit for drinking (liquors and the like), such a man is called पापः (*pāpaḥ*). Or, because a man who does evil deeds goes down into a lower state again and again, he is called पापः (*pāpaḥ*). Or the word पाप (*pāpa*) may be derived from the root पत् (*pat*) "to fall". This is the main sense of the nirvacana.
#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स॒म्राळ॒न्यः स्व॒राळ॒न्य उ॑च्यते वां म॒हांता॒विंद्रा॒वरु॑णा  ॒॑
> महावसू ।  
> विश्वे॑ दे॒वासः॑ पर॒मे व्यो॑मनि॒ सं वा॒मोजो॑ वृषणा॒ सं ॑ 
> बलं दधुः ॥ २ ॥

### Printed p. 13

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> संराट् । अन्यः । स्वराट् । अन्यः । उच्यते । वां । महांतौ । इंद्रावरुणा ।  
> महावसू इति महावसू ।  
> विश्वे । देवासः । परमे । विओमनि । सं । वां । ओजः । वृषणा । सं । बलं । दधुः॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> द्वितीयः पादः वरोक्षकृतः शिष्टाः प्रत्यक्षकृताः । हे इंद्रावरुणौ नां
> युवयोर्मध्येऽन्य एको वरुणः सम्राट् सम्यग्राजमान इत्युच्यते । ता सम्राजा धृतासुती । ऋ. २-४१-६ । इत्यादिषु कीर्त्यते । अन्य इंद्रः स्वराट् स्वयमेवान्यनिरपेक्षयैव राजमान इत्युच्यते । स्वराळिंद्रो दमे । ऋ. १-६१-९ । इत्यादिषु
> कीर्त्यते । तथाविधामिद्रावरुणेंद्रावरुणौ महांतौ गुणैरधिकौ महावसू
> महाधनौ च भवतः । हे वृषणा कामानां वर्षिताराविंद्रावरुणौ नां युवां
> परमे व्योमन्युत्कृष्ट आकाशे विश्वे देवासः सर्वॆ देवा ओजः सं दधुः। समयोजयन् । शरीरदाढ्याय । तद्धेतुभूतं तदोज इत्युच्यते । स्मर्यते च । ओजः
> साष्टमी दशेति । तथा बलं वृत्रवधादेः कार्यस्य हेतुभूतं सामर्थ्यं च युवाभ्यां सं दधुः । समयोजन् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The second pāda is in the third person (indirect address); the rest are in the direct address. O Indra and Varuṇa, of you two, one, Varuṇa, is called सम्राट् (*samrāṭ*), one who shines fully. He is so celebrated in passages such as ता सम्राजा धृतासुती (*tā samrājā dhṛtāsutī*) (Ṛ. 2-41-6). The other, Indra, is called स्वराट् (*svarāṭ*), one who shines of himself, independent of others. He is so celebrated in passages such as स्वराळिंद्रो दमे (*svarāḷiṃdro dame*) (Ṛ. 1-61-9). **NOTE**
Such Indra and Varuṇa are great (महांतौ (*mahāṃtau*)), greater in qualities, and have great wealth (महावसू (*mahāvasū*)). O showerers (वृषणा (*vṛṣaṇā*)) of desires, Indra and Varuṇa, in the highest sky (परमे व्योमनि (*parame vyomani*)) all the gods united ओजः (*ojaḥ*) for you (सं दधुः (*saṃ dadhuḥ*), "they joined"), for bodily firmness. The cause of that firmness is called ओजः (*ojaḥ*). It is also remembered: ओजः साष्टमी दशेति (*ojaḥ sāṣṭamī daśeti*). Likewise they united strength (बलं (*balaṃ*)), the capacity that is the cause of acts such as the slaying of Vṛtra, in you.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वां (*vāṃ*)—among you (O Indra and Varuṇa)
- अन्यः (*anyaḥ*)—one, Varuṇa
- सम्राट् (*samrāṭ*)—as a most radiant lord
- उच्यते (*ucyate*)—is celebrated
- अन्यः (*anyaḥ*)—the other, Indra
- स्वराट् (*svarāṭ*)—is celebrated as a self-luminous lord
- इंद्रावरुणा (*iṃdrāvaruṇā*)—such celebrated Indra and Varuṇa
- महांता (*mahāṃtā*)—are those of great qualities
- महावसू (*mahāvasū*)—and those of very abundant wealth
- वृषणा (*vṛṣaṇā*)—O Indra and Varuṇa, showerers of desires
- वां (*vāṃ*)—with you
- परमे व्योमनि (*parame vyomani*)—in the excellent sky
- विश्वे देवासः (*viśve devāsaḥ*)—all the gods
- ओजः (*ojaḥ*)—heroic vigour
- सं दधुः (*saṃ dadhuḥ*)—have united
- बलं (*balaṃ*)—and strength fit for slaying enemies
- सं (दधुः) (*saṃ (dadhuḥ)*)—have united

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra and Varuṇa, one of you, Varuṇa, is celebrated as a most radiant lord. The other, Indra, is celebrated as a self-luminous lord. Such celebrated Indra and Varuṇa are those of great qualities and of very abundant wealth. O Indra and Varuṇa, showerers of desires,
### Printed p. 14
all the gods too, in the excellent sky, have united with you vigour and strength fit for slaying enemies.

##### English translation
One of you is titled supreme monarch, so is the other : mighty and most opulent are you, Indra and Varuna : all the gods in the highest heaven, showerers (of benefits), have combined your united strength, (your) united vigour.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In this Ṛk, in praising Indra and Varuṇa, they are praised by the words अन्यः सम्राट् अन्यः स्वराट् (*anyaḥ samrāṭ anyaḥ svarāṭ*). In this matter the commentator makes it clear by two examples: that Varuṇa is praised by the word सम्राट् (*samrāṭ*) in the Ṛk
ता सम्राजा धृतासुती आदित्या दानुनस्पती । सचेते अनवह्वरं ॥ (*tā samrājā dhṛtāsutī ādityā dānunaspatī | sacete anavahvaraṃ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-42-6)
and that Indra is praised by the word स्वराट् (*svarāṭ*) in the Ṛk
स्वराळिंद्रो दम आ विश्वगूर्तः स्वरिरमत्तो ववक्षे रणाय । (*svarāḷiṃdro dama ā viśvagūrtaḥ svariramatto vavakṣe raṇāya |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-61-9)
#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अन्व॒पां खान्य॑तृंत॒मोज॒सा सूर्य॑मैरयतं दि॒वि प्र॒भुं ।  
> इंद्रा॑वरुणा॒ मदे॑ अस्य मा॒यिनोपि॑न्वतम॒पितः॒ पिन्व॑तं॒  ॑
> धियः ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अनु । अपां । खानि । अतृंतं । ओजसा । आ । सूर्यं । ऐरयतं । दिवि । प्रभुं ।  
> इंद्रावरुणा । मदे । अस्य । मायिनः । अपिन्वतं । अपितः । पिन्वतं । धियः ॥ ३ ॥

### Printed p. 15

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्रावरुण् अपामुदकानां खानि द्वाराणि वृत्रेण पिहितान्योजसा
> बलेनान्वत्यंतं । अन्वविध्यतं । अवरकस्य वृत्रस्य वधेन वृष्टिप्रतिबंधं निराकृतवंतावित्यर्थः । तथा सूर्यं सर्वस्य प्रेरकमादित्यं दिव्यंतरिक्षे प्रभुं प्रभूतं संतमैरयतं । अभ्यगमयतु । स्वर्भानुनावृतं सूर्यं तद्वधेन प्रकाशितवंतावित्यर्थः । हे इंद्रावरुणेंद्रावरुणौ मायिनः प्रज्ञाकरस्यास्य सोमस्य पानेन
> मदे हर्ष सत्यपितो जलरहिता नदीरपिन्वतं । जलेनापूरयतं । तथा च निवित्वदमाम्नायते । अस्य मदे जरितरिंद्रोऽजिन्वदजुवोऽपिन्वदपित इति । अपि च धियः
> कर्माण्यस्माभिरनुष्ठितानि कर्माणि पिन्वतं । सिंचतं । फलेन पूरयतं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra and Varuṇa, the doors (खानि (*khāni*)) of the waters, closed by Vṛtra, you pierced through (*anvatṛṃtaṃ*, अन्वविध्यतं (*anvavidhyataṃ*)) with your strength (ओजसा (*ojasā*)). The sense is that by slaying the base Vṛtra you removed the obstruction to rain. Likewise you caused (ऐरयतं (*airayataṃ*), अभ्यगमयतु (*abhyagamayatu*)) Sūrya, the impeller of all, Āditya, to be a lord (प्रभुं (*prabhuṃ*)) in the heavenly sky. The sense is that Sūrya, who had been covered by Svarbhānu, you made to shine by slaying him. O Indra and Varuṇa, in the exhilaration (मदे (*made*)) arising from drinking this wisdom-giving (मायिनः (*māyinaḥ*)) Soma, you filled with water the rivers that were waterless (अपितः (*apitaḥ*); अपिन्वतं (*apinvataṃ*)). So the nivid text is recited: अस्य मदे जरितरिंद्रोऽजिन्वदजुवोऽपिन्वदपित इति (*asya made jaritariṃdro 'jinvadajuvo 'pinvadapita iti*). Moreover, the rites performed by us (धियः (*dhiyaḥ*) means rites), do you पिन्वतं (*pinvataṃ*), that is, sprinkle, and fill with fruit.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अपां (*apāṃ*)—of the waters (O Indra and Varuṇa)
- खानि (*khāni*)—the doors (closed by Vṛtra)
- ओजसा (*ojasā*)—by your vigour
- अनु अतृंतं (*anu atṛṃtaṃ*)—you opened out completely
- सूर्यं (*sūryaṃ*)—Sūrya
- दिवि (*divi*)—in the sky
- प्रभुं (*prabhuṃ*)—as lord
- आ ऐरयतं (*ā airayataṃ*)—you have established
- इंद्रावरुणा (*iṃdrāvaruṇā*)—O Indra and Varuṇa
- मायिनः (*māyinaḥ*)—life-giving
- अस्य (*asya*)—of this, arising from the drinking of Soma
- मदे (*made*)—as the result of the exhilaration
- अपितः (*apitaḥ*)—the waterless rivers
- अपिन्वतं (*apinvataṃ*)—you made full of water
- धियः (*dhiyaḥ*)—our rites
- पिन्वतं (*pinvataṃ*)—you made full of fruit

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra and Varuṇa, you opened out completely, by your vigour, the doors of the waters closed by Vṛtra. It is you who regulated and established Sūrya as lord in the sky. O Indra and Varuṇa, as the result of the exhilaration arising from drinking this life-giving Soma, you made the waterless rivers full of water. You made our rites full of fruit.

##### English translation
Indra and Varuna, you have forced open by your strength the barriers of the waters: you have established Surya as the lord in heaven : in the exhilaration of the inspiring (Soma) you have replenished the dry (beds of the rivers) : do you also fulfil the objects of our sacred rites.

### Printed p. 16
#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यु॒वामिद्यु॒त्तु पृत॑नासु॒ वह्न॑यो यु॒वां क्षेम॑स्य प्रस॒वे  ॒॑
> मितज्ञवः ।  
> ई॒शा॒ना वस्व॑ उ॒भय॑स्य का॒रव॒ इंद्रा॑वरुणा सु॒हवा॑ हवामहे ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> युवां । इत् । युत्सु । पृतनासु । वह्नयः । युवां । क्षेमस्य । प्रसवे ।  
> मितज्ञवः ।  
> ईशाना । स्वः । उभयस्य । कारवः । इंद्रावरुणा । सुहवा । हवामहे ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्रावरुणौ वह्नयो हविषां स्तोत्राणां ना वोढार ऋत्विजो युत्तु
> युद्धेषु पृतनासु शत्रुसेनासु रक्षणार्थं युवामिद्युवामेव हवंते । आह्वयंति । मितज्ञवः संकुचितजानुका अंगिरसोऽपि क्षेमस्य रक्षणस्य प्रसव उत्पादने
> निमित्तभूते सति युवामेव हवंते । अतः कारणात् हे इंद्रावरुणौ कारवः
> स्तोतारो वयमप्युभयस्य दिव्यस्य पार्थिवस्य च वस्टो वसुनो धनस्येशानेश्वरौ
> सुहवा सुखेन ह्वातव्यौ युवामेव हवामहे । आह्वयामहे ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Addressing Indra and Varuṇa, Sāyaṇa says that the वह्नयः (*vahnayaḥ*), the carriers of the oblations and hymns, that is, the priests, invoke the two of them alone in battles, in the hostile armies, for protection. The Aṅgirases, the मितज्ञवः (*mitajñavaḥ*) who kneel with bent knees, likewise invoke the two of them alone when the begetting of protection (क्षेमस्य प्रसवे (*kṣemasya prasave*)) is the occasion. For this reason the singers (कारवः (*kāravaḥ*)), "we" too, invoke the two of them alone, who are the lords (ईशाना (*īśānā*)) of the wealth of both kinds, celestial and terrestrial, and who are easy to invoke (सुहवा (*suhavā*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (ऎलै इंद्रावरुणरे (*èlai iṃdrāvaruṇare*)) वह्नयः (*vahnayaḥ*)—priests who carry the oblations
- युत्तु (*yuttu*)—engaged in battles
- पृतनासु (*pṛtanāsu*)—for the protection of the armies
- युवां इत् (*yuvāṃ it*)—you alone (they call)
- मितज्ञवः (*mitajñavaḥ*)—the Aṅgirases also, who pray kneeling on their knees
- क्षेमस्य (*kṣemasya*)—of protection
- प्रसवे (*prasave*)—for the sake of
- युवां (*yuvāṃ*)—you alone they call
- इंद्रावरुणा (*iṃdrāvaruṇā*)—(therefore) O Indra and Varuṇa
- कारवर् (*kāravar*)—we also, the singers
- उभयस्य (*ubhayasya*)—of both, the world of heaven and the world of earth
- वस्वः (*vasvaḥ*)—of wealth
- ईशाना (*īśānā*)—you who are the lords
- सुहवा (*suhavā*)—you, who are worthy of easy invocation
- हवामहे (*havāmahe*)—we pray to and invoke

### Printed p. 17

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra and Varuṇa, the priests who carry the oblations invoke you alone for the protection of the armies engaged in battles. The Aṅgirases also, who pray kneeling on their knees, invoke you alone for the sake of protection. Therefore, O Indra and Varuṇa, we too, the singers, pray to and invoke you alone, who are the lords of the wealth of both the world of heaven and the world of earth, and who are worthy of easy invocation.

##### English translation
The bearers (of offerings) invoke you, Indra and Varuna, in wars, in battles: on bended knees (the Angirasas invoke you) for the begetting of prosperity; and we, your worshippers, invoke you, who are entitled to respectful homage, the lords of both celestial and terrestrial treasures.
#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इंद्रा॑वरुणा॒ यदि॒मानि॑ च॒क्रथु॒र्विश्वा॑ जा॒तानि॒ भुव॑नस्य  ॒॑
> मज्मना ।  
> क्षेमे॑ण मि॒त्रो वरु॑णं दुव॒स्यति॑ म॒रुद्भि॑रु॒ग्रः शुभ॑म॒न्य  ॑
> ईयते ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ंद्रावरुणा । यत् । इमानि । चक्रथुः । विश्वा । जातानि । भुवनस्य । मज्मना ।  
> क्षेमेण । मित्रः । वरुणं । दुवस्यति । मरुद्भिः । उग्रः । शुभं । अन्यः । ईयते॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्रावरुणौ यद्यौ युवां भुवनस्य लोकस्य संबंधिनीमानि परिदृश्यमानानि विश्वा सर्वाणि जातानि जातिमंति भूतजातानि मज्मनात्मीयेन बलेन चक्रथुः
> कृतवंतौ तयोर्युवयोर्मध्य एकं वरुणं क्षेमेण रक्षणहेतुना मित्रो
> देवो दुवस्यति । परिचरति । मित्रावरुणौ हि परस्परं प्राप्तसख्यौ । अत एव सहचरा
> 2                                                                    [ Vol. 23 ]

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra and Varuṇa, since (यद्यौ (*yadyau*)) you two have made by your own might (मज्मना (*majmanā*)) all these visible creatures (जातानि (*jātāni*)) belonging to the world (भुवनस्य (*bhuvanasya*)), then of the two of you the god Mitra serves (दुवस्यति (*duvasyati*)) the one, Varuṇa, for the sake of protection (क्षेमेण (*kṣemeṇa*)). Mitra and Varuṇa are indeed friends of each other, and for that very reason they are seen going together.

### Printed p. 19

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> दृश्येते । अन्य एक इंद्रो मरुद्भिर्मरुद्धणैरुग्र उद्धूर्णबलः सञ्छभुं शोभनमलंकारमीयते । प्राप्नोति । यद्वा । मरुद्भिर्मध्यस्थानैर्देवगणैः सार्धमुग्र
> ओजस्तींद्रः शुभमुदकमीयते । प्रेरयति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The other one, Indra, together with the Maruts, the hosts of gods, is fierce (उग्र (*ugra*)), of surging strength, and being so attains (ईयते (*īyate*)) a splendid ornament (शुभं (*śubhaṃ*)). Or else: Indra, fierce in his might together with the Maruts, the gods of the middle region, sends forth (ईयते (*īyate*) means प्रेरयति (*prerayati*)) fair water.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्रावरुणा (*iṃdrāvaruṇā*)—O Indra and Varuṇa
- यत् (*yat*)—you who
- भुवनस्य (*bhuvanasya*)—belonging to the whole world
- विश्वा (*viśvā*)—all
- इमा जातानि (*imā jātāni*)—these creatures
- मज्मना (*majmanā*)—by your power
- चक्रथुः (*cakrathuḥ*)—have produced (of the two of you, one)
- वरुणं (*varuṇaṃ*)—Varuṇa
- क्षेमेण (*kṣemeṇa*)—for protection
- मित्र: (*mitra:*)—the god Mitra
- दुवस्यति (*duvasyati*)—worships
- अन्यः (*anyaḥ*)—the other, Indra
- मरुद्धिः (*maruddhiḥ*)—together with the Maruts
- उग्रः (*ugraḥ*)—being endowed with exceeding power
- शुभं (*śubhaṃ*)—an excellent ornament
- ईयते (*īyate*)—obtains

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra and Varuṇa, you who have produced by your power all these creatures belonging to the whole world: of the two of you, Mitra worships Varuṇa for protection. The other, Indra, together with the Maruts, endowed with exceeding power, obtains an excellent ornament; or else he sends forth excellent rain-water.

##### English translation
Indra and Varuna, inasmuch as you have created by your might all these beings of the world, therefore Mitra worships Varuna for prospe- rity, whilst the other, the fierce Indra associated with the Maruts, acquires glory.
#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> म॒हे शु॒ल्काय॒ वरु॑णस्य॒ नु त्वि॒ष ओजो॑ मिमाते ध्रु॒वम॑स्॒त  
> यत्स्वं ।  
> अजा॑मिम॒न्यः शृ॒थय॑न्त॒माति॑रद्द॒भ्रेभि॑र॒न्यः प्र वृ॑णोति॒  ॑
> भूयसः ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> महे । शुल्काय । वरुणस्य । नु । त्विषे । ओजः । मिमाते इति । ध्रुवं ' अस्य ।  
> यत् । स्वं ।  
> अजामिं । अन्यः । श्नथयन्तं । आ । अतिरत् । दभ्रेभिः । अन्यः । प्र । वृणोति ।  
> भूयसः ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> वरुणस्य । उपलक्षणमेतत् । इंद्रस्य वरुणस्य च त्विषे दीप्त्यर्थमोजो
> बलं नु क्षिप्रं मिमाते । स्तोत्रेण निर्मिमाते यजमानपत्न्यौ । स्तोत्रेण हि बलं
> जायते । किमर्थं । महे महते शुल्काय धनाय । ईदृशस्य धनस्य लाभार्थं ।
> अस्येंद्रस्य वरुणस्य च ध्रुवं नित्यं स्वं स्वकीयमसाधारणं यदोजो
> विद्यते तदोजो मिमाते इत्यन्वयः । तयोरिंद्रावरुणयोरन्य एको वरुणोऽजामिमबंधुमस्तुवंतं श्नथयंतं हिंसंतं कर्माण्यकुर्वंतमस्तोतारमयजमानं चातिरत् । अभिहंति । अन्य एक इंद्रो दभ्रेभिरल्पैरेवोपाय्कॆर्भूयसो बहुतराञ्छत्रून् वृणोति । प्रकर्षणावृतान् बाधितान् करोति । यद्वा ।
> भूयसो यजमानान्नृ वृणोति । प्रवरानुत्कृष्टान् करोति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

वरुणस्य (*varuṇasya*) is only indicative: it means "of Indra and of Varuṇa". For radiance (त्विषे (*tviṣe*)) the sacrificer and his wife quickly (नु (*nu*)) fashion (मिमाते (*mimāte*)) strength (ओजः (*ojaḥ*)) by praise, for strength is born of praise. For what purpose? For great (महे (*mahe*)) wealth (शुल्काय (*śulkāya*)), to gain such wealth. The construction is: they fashion that strength which is constant, their own and unshared, belonging to Indra and Varuṇa. Of these two, the one, Varuṇa, overcomes (अतिरत् (*atirat*), he strikes down) the unkindred (अजामिं (*ajāmiṃ*), one who is no kinsman, who does not praise), who harms (श्नथयंतं (*śnathayaṃtaṃ*)), who performs no rites, the non-singer, the non-sacrificer. The other, Indra, with scanty means (दभ्रेभिः (*dabhrebhiḥ*)) alone, प्र वृणोति (*pra vṛṇoti*) the more numerous (भूयसः (*bhūyasaḥ*)) enemies: he makes them thoroughly hemmed in, hindered. Or else: he chooses the more numerous sacrificers and makes them excellent, pre-eminent.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- महे (*mahe*)—great
- शुल्काय (*śulkāya*)—for wealth
- वरुणस्य (*varuṇasya*)—of Indra and Varuṇa
- त्वषे (*tvaṣe*)—to increase the splendour
- नु (*nu*)—quickly
- अस्य (*asya*)—for these Indra and Varuṇa
- ध्रुवं (*dhruvaṃ*)—constant
- स्वं (*svaṃ*)—and peculiar to each of them
- यत् (*yat*)—whatever
- ओजः (*ojaḥ*)—vigour there is, that vigour
- मिमाते (*mimāte*)—the sacrificer and his wife produce by praises and the like
- अन्यः (*anyaḥ*)—one of these, Varuṇa
- अजामिं (*ajāmiṃ*)—one who is not a praiser but stays far away
- शृथयंतं (*śṛthayaṃtaṃ*)—and is a harmer, one fallen from the rite
- अतिरत् (*atirat*)—destroys
- अन्यः (*anyaḥ*)—the other, Indra
- दभ्रेभिः (*dabhrebhiḥ*)—with scanty means alone
- भूयसः (*bhūyasaḥ*)—the more numerous enemies
- प्र वृणोति (*pra vṛṇoti*)—destroys completely

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

For the sake of great wealth, to increase the splendour of Indra and Varuṇa, the sacrificer and his wife produce by praises and the like that constant vigour, peculiar to each, which belongs to these two. Of them, Varuṇa destroys the one fallen from the rite, who does not praise him but keeps far away and is a harmer. The other, Indra, with scanty means alone completely destroys the more numerous enemies.

### Printed p. 20

##### English translation
In honour of (Indra and Varuna) the sacrificer and his wife for (the obtaining of ) great wealth, confer (by their praises) that strength which is peculiar to each of them, and ever-enduring ; one of them (Varuna) destroys the unfriendly man neglecting (his worship): the other, (Indra), with scanty (means), discomfits numerous (enemies).
#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> न तमंहो॒ न दु॑रि॒तानि॒ मर्त्य॒मिंद्रा॑वरुणा॒ न तपः॒ कुत॑श्च॒न ।  
> यस्य॑ देवा॒ गच ॑थो वी॒थो अ॑ध्व॒रं न तं मर्त॑स्य नशते॒  ॑
> परिह्वतिः ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> न । तं । अंहः । न । दुः इतानि । मर्त्यं । इंद्रावरुणा । न । तपः । कुतः । चन ।  
> यस्य । देवा । गच्छथः । वीथः । अध्वरं । न । तं । मर्तस्य । नशते । परिह्वृतिः ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्रावरुणेंद्रावरुणौ तं मर्त्यं मनुष्यमंहः पापं न नशते । न
> व्याप्नोति । न च दुरितानि दुर्गमनानि पापफलानि च प्राप्नुवंति : कुतश्चन कस्मादपि
> निमित्तात्तपः संतापश्च तं न प्राप्नोति । हे देवा देवौ दानादिगुणयुक्तानिंद्रावरुणौ यस्य मर्त्यस्य मनुष्यस्याध्वरं यज्ञं गच्छथः प्राप्नुथो युवां वीथः
> कामयेथे च यस्य हवींषि तं मनुष्यं परिह्वतिः परिबाधा न नशते । उक्तप्रकारेण न प्राप्नोति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra and Varuṇa, sin (अंहः (*aṃhaḥ*)) does not reach (नशते (*naśate*) means व्याप्नोति (*vyāpnoti*)) that mortal (मर्त्यं (*martyaṃ*)), nor do evil-goings (दुरितानि (*duritāni*)), the fruits of sin, reach him; nor does तपः (*tapaḥ*), affliction, from any cause whatever (कुतश्चन (*kutaścana*)). O gods, Indra and Varuṇa, endowed with qualities such as generosity: whose sacrifice (अध्वरं (*adhvaraṃ*)) you two attend (गच्छथः (*gacchathaḥ*)) and whose oblations you desire (वीथः (*vīthaḥ*)), that man is not reached by परिह्वतिः (*parihvatiḥ*), harassment, in the manner stated.

### Printed p. 21

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- देवा (*devā*)—you who are divine in nature
- इंद्रावरुणा (*iṃdrāvaruṇā*)—O Indra and Varuṇa
- यस्य (*yasya*)—whose
- मर्तस्य (*martasya*)—man's
- अध्वरं (*adhvaraṃ*)—sacrifice
- गच्छथः (*gacchathaḥ*)—you go to
- वीधः (*vīdhaḥ*)—and desire and favour it
- तं मर्त्यं (*taṃ martyaṃ*)—that sacrificer
- अ०हः (*a0haḥ*)—sin
- न नशते (*na naśate*)—does not cling to
- दुरितानि (*duritāni*)—the fruits of sin also
- न (*na*)—do not cling
- कुतश्चन (*kutaścana*)—of whatever kind
- तपः (*tapaḥ*)—affliction also
- न (*na*)—does not cling
- तं (*taṃ*)—that same man
- परिहृतिः (*parihṛtiḥ*)—harm also
- न (*na*)—does not approach

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra and Varuṇa, who are divine in nature: neither sin nor the fruits of sin cling to that sacrificer to whose sacrifice you go and which you desire and favour out of regard. No affliction of any kind clings to him. No harm approaches that man.

##### English translation
Sin contaminates not, difficulties assail not, nor distress at any time afflicts the mortal, Indra and Varuna, to whose sacrifices you, deities, repair, and of which you approve : such a man ruin, never destroys.
#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ॒र्वाङ्न॑रा॒ दैव्ये॒नाव॒सा ग॑तं शृणु॒तं हवं॒ यदि॑ मे॒  ॑
> जुजोषथः ।  
> यु॒वोर्हि स॒ख्यमु॒त वा॒ यदाप्यं॑ मार्डि॒कमिं॑द्रावरुणा॒  ॑
> नि यच्छतं ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अर्वाक् । नरा । दैव्येन । अवसा । आ । गतं । शृणुतं । हवं । यदि । मे ।  
> जुजोषथः ।  
> युवोः । हि । सख्यं । उत । वा । यत् । आप्यं । मार्डिकं । इंद्रावरुणा । नि ।  
> यच्छतं ॥ ८ ॥

### Printed p. 22

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे नरा नेताराविंद्रावरुणौ दैव्येन देवसंबंधिनावसा रक्षणेन सहर्वागस्मदभिमुखमा गतं । आगच्छतं । अगत्य च मदीयं हवं स्तोत्रं शृणुतं । यदि
> मे मम जुजोषथः प्रीयेथे । मयि प्रीतिरस्ति चेत् युवाभ्यामागंतव्यं
> मदीयं स्तोत्रं श्रोतव्यं च भवतीत्यर्थः । युवोर्हि युवयोः खलु
> यत्सख्यं सखित्वमुत वापि च यदाप्यमापित्वं बांधवं मार्डिकं मृडीकस्य
> सुखस्य साधनं तदुभयं हे इंद्रावरुणौ नि यच्छतं । अस्मभ्यं प्रयच्छतं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O leaders (नरा (*narā*)), Indra and Varuṇa, come (आ गतं (*ā gataṃ*)) toward us (अर्वाक् (*arvāk*)) with divine (दैव्येन (*daivyena*)) protection (अवसा (*avasā*)), and having come, hear my invocation, that is, my hymn. यदि मे जुजोषथः (*yadi me jujoṣathaḥ*): if you take pleasure in me, that is, if there is affection for me, then you must come and my hymn must be heard; this is the meaning. Your friendship (सख्यं (*sakhyaṃ*)) and your kinship (आप्यं (*āpyaṃ*)), which is the means of मृडीक (*mṛḍīka*), of happiness (मार्डिकं (*mārḍikaṃ*)): both of these, O Indra and Varuṇa, grant (नि यच्छतं (*ni yacchataṃ*)) to us.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- नरा (*narā*)—O Indra and Varuṇa, leaders
- दैव्येन (*daivyena*)—belonging to the gods
- अवसा (*avasā*)—with protection
- अर्वाक् (*arvāk*)—toward us
- आ गतं (*ā gataṃ*)—be gracious and come
- इंद्रा वरुणा (*iṃdrā varuṇā*)—O Indra and Varuṇa
- यदि (*yadi*)—if
- मे (*me*)—in me
- जुजोषथः (*jujoṣathaḥ*)—you have placed affection
- हवं (*havaṃ*)—our hymn
- शृणुतं (*śṛṇutaṃ*)—hear
- युवोः हि (*yuvoḥ hi*)—of you two indeed
- सख्यं (*sakhyaṃ*)—the friendship
- उत वा (*uta vā*)—and moreover
- यत् (*yat*)—which
- आप्यं (*āpyaṃ*)—your kinship
- मार्डिकं (*mārḍikaṃ*)—is the means of happiness, that also
- नि यच्छतं (*ni yacchataṃ*)—graciously grant to us

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O leaders, Indra and Varuṇa, be gracious and come toward us with divine protection. If you have affection for me, hear our praise. Graciously grant us your friendship and your kinship, which is the means of happiness.

##### English translation
Leaders of rites, come to our presence with divine protection : if you have any regard for me, hear my invocation: verily your friendship, your affinity, is the source of happiness: grant them, Indra and Varuna, (unto us).
#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒स्माक॑मिंद्रावरुणा॒ भरे॑भरे पुरोयो॒धा भ॑वतं  
> कृष्टोऽजसा ।

### Printed p. 23

> यद्वां हवंत उभये अध स्पृधि नरस्तोकस्य तनयस्य  
> सातिषु ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अस्माकं । इंद्रावरुणा । भरेऽभरे । पुरःऽयोधा । भवतं । कृष्टिऽजसा ।  
> यत् । वां । हवंते । उभये । अध । स्पृधि । नरः । तोकस्य । तनयस्य । सातिषु ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे कृष्णोजसा शत्रूणां कर्षकमोजो बलं ययोस्तादृशौ हे इंद्रावरुणौ भरे भरे संग्रामे संग्रामेऽस्माकं पुरोयोधा पुरस्ताद्योद्धारौ
> भवतं । यदौ वामुभय उभयविधाः पूर्वकालीना इदानींतनाश्च नरो
> नेतारः स्तोतारः स्पृधि युद्धे यौ युवां हवंते । आधापि च तोकस्य पुत्रस्य
> तनयस्य पौत्रस्य सातिषु संभजनीयेषु निमित्तभूतेषु च यौ युवां हवंते ।
> तावस्माकं पुरोयोधा भवतमित्यन्वयः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra and Varuṇa, whose strength (ओजः (*ojaḥ*)) draws in (*karṣakaṃ*) the enemies (कृष्टि (*kṛṣṭi*)): in every battle (भरे भरे (*bhare bhare*)) become our front-fighters (पुरोयोधा (*puroyodhā*)). The two kinds of men, those of former times and those of the present, the leaders and singers, who invoke you two in strife (स्पृधि (*spṛdhi*)), in battle, and moreover who invoke you on occasions of winning (सातिषु (*sātiṣu*)), occasions to be shared and productive of the benefit, of a son (तोकस्य (*tokasya*)) and a grandson (तनयस्य (*tanayasya*)): you two, thus invoked, are to become our front-fighters. This is the construction.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- कृष्णोजसा (*kṛṣṇojasā*)—you who have the power to seize and drag in the enemies
- इंद्रावरुणा (*iṃdrāvaruṇā*)—O Indra and Varuṇa
- यत् वां (*yat vāṃ*)—you two whom
- उभये नरः (*ubhaye naraḥ*)—the former singers and likewise the present singers
- स्पृधि (*spṛdhi*)—in battle
- हवंते (*havaṃte*)—are invoking
- अध (*adha*)—and moreover
- तोकस्य (*tokasya*)—of a son
- तनयस्य (*tanayasya*)—of his son
- सातिषु (*sātiṣu*)—are invoking for protection also (you two, such as these)
- भरे भरे (*bhare bhare*)—in every battle
- अस्माकं (*asmākaṃ*)—our
- पुरोयोधा (*puroyodhā*)—fighters standing in front
- भवतं (*bhavataṃ*)—be

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra and Varuṇa, who have the power to seize and drag in the enemies: you two whom the former singers, and likewise the present singers, invoke in battle, and also invoke for the protection of their sons, grandsons and the like: such as you are, be in every battle fighters standing in front of us.

### Printed p. 24

##### English translation
Indra and Varuna, of irresistible strength, be our preceders in every encounter, for both (past and present) worshippers invoke you to defend them in war, or for the acquirement of sons and grandsons.

#### Ṛk 10

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒स्मे इंद्रो॒ वरु॑णो मि॒त्रो अ॑र्य॒मा द्यु॒न्नुं य॑च्छंतु॒ ॒ ॒॑॑
> महि॒ शर्म॑ स॒प्रथः॑ ।  
> अ॒व॒ध्रं ज्योति॒रदि॑तेर्ऋता॒वृधो॑ दे॒वस्य॒ शोकं॑ सवि॒तुर्म॑नामहे ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अस्मे इति । इंद्रः । वरुणः । मित्रः । अर्यमा । द्युम्नं । यच्छंतु । महि ।  
> शर्म । सप्रथः ।  
> अवध्रं । ज्योतिः । अदितेः । ऋतवृधः । देवस्य । श्लोकं । सवितुः । मनामहे ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अस्मे अस्मभ्यमिंद्रादयो द्युम्नं द्योतमानं धनं यच्छंतु । प्रयच्छंतु।
> तथा महि महत्सप्रथः सर्वतः पृथु विस्तीर्णं शर्म गृहं च प्रयच्छंतु । अपि
> चर्तावृध ऋतस्य यज्ञस्य वर्धयित्र्या अदितेरदीनाया देवमातुर्ज्योतिस्तेजश्च
> नोऽस्माकमवध्रमहिंसकमस्तु । वयं च देवस्यदानादिगुणयुक्तस्य सवितुः सर्वस्य
> प्रेरकस्य श्लोकं स्तोत्रं मनामहे । जानीमः । कुर्म इत्यर्थः । यद्वा। देवेन
> सवित्रास्मभ्यं देयं श्लोकं यशो मनामहे । याचामहे ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

May Indra and the other deities grant us shining wealth, and also a great, widely extended house. May the radiance of Aditi, the mother of the gods, who makes the sacrifice (ṛta) grow, be unharmed for us, that is, harmless. We on our part मनामहे (*manāmahe*) the praise of the god Savitṛ, who is endowed with qualities such as generosity and is the impeller of all. The word means "we know", that is, "we make". Alternatively: we ask for the fame (श्लोकं (*ślokaṃ*) = glory) that the god Savitṛ is to give us.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- वरुणः (*varuṇaḥ*)—Varuṇa
- मित्रः (*mitraḥ*)—Mitra
- अर्यमा (*aryamā*)—Aryaman
- अस्मे (*asme*)—to us
- द्युम्नं (*dyumnaṃ*)—wealth
- यच्छंतु (*yacchaṃtu*)—may they give
- महि (*mahi*)—great
- सप्रथः (*saprathaḥ*)—very extensive

### Printed p. 25
- शर्म (*śarma*)—a house, may they give
- ऋता वृधः (*ṛtā vṛdhaḥ*)—of Aditi, who makes the sacrifice grow
- अदितेः (*aditeḥ*)—of Aditi
- ज्योतिः (*jyotiḥ*)—the radiance
- अवध्रं (*avadhraṃ*)—may it be unharmed for us
- देवस्य (*devasya*)—of the one whose self is divine
- सवितुः (*savituḥ*)—of Savitṛ
- श्लोकु (*śloku*)—the praise
- मनामहे (*manāmahe*)—we meditate on and compose

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

May Indra, Varuṇa, Mitra and Aryaman give us wealth. May they also give a great and very extensive house. May the radiance of Aditi, who makes the sacrifice grow, be unharmed for us. We meditate on and compose the praise of Savitṛ, whose self is divine, and we recite it.

##### English translation
May Indra, Varuna, Mitra, and Aryaman grant us wealth and a large and spacious habitation; may the lustre of Aditi, the augmentress of sacrifice, be innocuous to us: we recite the praise of the divine Savitri.

## Sūkta 83 (Maṇḍala 7)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> युवां नरेति दशर्चं त्रयोदशं सूक्तं वसिष्ठस्यार्षं जागतमैंद्रावरुणं । युवां नरेत्यनुक्रांतं ॥ अभिप्लविकेषूक्ष्येषु स्तोमवृद्धौ प्रशास्तुरिदं
> सूक्तमावापार्थं । सूत्रितं च । युवां नरा पुनीषे वां । आ. ७.९ । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sūkta beginning युवां नरा (*yuvāṃ narā*) has ten ṛks and is the thirteenth sūkta of the anuvāka. Its seer is Vasiṣṭha, its deity Indra and Varuṇa, its metre Jagatī; the Anukramaṇī says युवां नरेति (*yuvāṃ nareti*). In the Abhiplava-related Ukthya rites, when the stoma is to be increased, the priest called Praśāstṛ recites this sūkta, and the Sūtra (Āśvalāyana) prescribes it: युवां नरा पुनीषे वां (*yuvāṃ narā punīṣe vāṃ*) (Ā. 7-9).

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta, "Yuvaṃ narā", is the thirteenth sūkta in the fifth anuvāka. It has ten ṛks. Vasiṣṭha is the seer of this sūkta; Indra and Varuṇa are the deity; the metre is Jagatī. In the Anukramaṇī it is said: "Yuvaṃ narā". The Aśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra, in the sūtra "Yuvaṃ narā punīṣe vāṃ" (Ā. 7-9), explains that the priest called Praśāstṛ must recite this sūkta for the increase of the stoma in the Ukthya sacrifice connected with the Abhiplava.

Sūkta—83
Maṇḍala—7; Anuvāka—5; Sūkta—83
Aṣṭaka—5; Adhyāya—6; Varga—4, 5
Number of ṛks in the sūkta—10
Ṛṣi—Vasiṣṭha
Devatā—Indra and Varuṇa
Chandas—Jagatī

### Printed p. 26
#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यु॒वां न॑रा॒ पश्य॑मानास॒ आप्यं॑ प्रा॒चा ग॒व्यंतः॑ सृथु॒पर्श॑वो ययुः ।  
> दासा॑ च वृ॒त्रा ह॒तमार्या॑णि च सु॒दास॑मिंद्रावरु॒णाव॑सावतं ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> युवां । नरा । पश्यमानासः । आप्यं । प्राचा । गव्यंतः । पृथुपर्शवः । ययुः ।  
> दासा ! च । वृत्रा । हतं । आर्याणि । च । सुदासं । इंद्रावरुणा । अवसा ।  
> अवतं ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे नरा नेताराविंद्रावरुणौ युवां । षष्ठ्यर्थे द्वितीया । युवयोराप्यं बंधुभावं पश्यमानासः पश्यंतो युष्मद्वांधवलाभार्थिनो गव्यंतो गा
> आत्मन इच्छंतो यजमानाः पृथुपर्शनः । पृथुर्विस्तीर्णः पर्शुः पार्श्वास्थि
> येषां ते तथोक्ताः । विस्तीर्णाश्वपर्शुहस्ताः संतः प्राचा प्राचीनं ययुः ।
> बर्हिराहरणार्थं गच्छंति । पर्श्वा हि बर्हिराच्छिद्यते । तथा च तैत्तिरीयकं । अश्वपर्श्वा बर्हिराच्छ्यतीति । हे इंद्रावरुणौ युवां दासा दासान्युपक्षपयित्रिणि च
> वृत्राण्यावरकाणि शत्रुजातान्यार्याणि च कर्मानुष्ठानपराणि च शत्रुजातानि हतं ।
> हिंस्तं । अपि च सुदासमस्मद्याज्यमेतत्संज्ञं राजानमवसा रक्षणेन सार्धमवतं ।
> आगच्छतं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O leaders Indra and Varuṇa, युवां (*yuvāṃ*) stands in the accusative for the genitive sense, "of you". The sacrificers, seeing (पश्यमानासः (*paśyamānāsaḥ*)) your kinship, desiring your friendship and wishing cattle for themselves, are पृथुपर्शवः (*pṛthuparśavaḥ*): पृथु (*pṛthu*) is broad, पर्शुः (*parśuḥ*) is a rib-bone (a hatchet-like tool); they have broad horse-rib hatchets in hand, and go प्राचा (*prācā*), to the east, to fetch the sacrificial grass, since the grass is cut with a hatchet. So the Taittirīya: अश्वपर्श्वा बर्हिराच्छ्यतीति (*aśvaparśvā barhirācchyatīti*). O Indra and Varuṇa, kill the enemy hosts, both of the Dāsas, who destroy the people, and of the Āryas who are intent on performing rites, which are the obstructing (वृत्रा (*vṛtrā*)) enemy groups. Come also with protection to Sudāsa, the king, my patron of that name.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- नरा (*narā*)—O Indra and Varuṇa, leaders
- युवां (*yuvāṃ*)—your
- आप्यं (*āpyaṃ*)—kinship
- पश्यमानासः (*paśyamānāsaḥ*)—having looked to
- गव्यंतः (*gavyaṃtaḥ*)—the sacrificers who desire cattle
- पृथुपर्शवः (*pṛthuparśavaḥ*)—holding big hatchets
- प्राचा (*prācā*)—in the eastern direction
- ययुः (*yayuḥ*)—have set out
- इंद्रावरुणा (*iṃdrāvaruṇā*)—O Indra and Varuṇa
- दासा च आर्याणि च वृत्रा (*dāsā ca āryāṇi ca vṛtrā*)—the enemies belonging to the group of Dāsas and the enemies belonging to the group of Ātmas
- हतं (*hataṃ*)—destroy
- सुदासु (*sudāsu*)—to the king named Sudāsa
- अवसा (*avasā*)—together with protection
- अवतं (*avataṃ*)—come

### Printed p. 27

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra and Varuṇa, leaders, the sacrificers, who look for your kinship and help and desire cattle, have set out to the east with big hatchets to cut the darbha grass. O Indra and Varuṇa, destroy the enemies belonging to the group of Dāsas and those belonging to the group of Āryas. Come with your protection to the king named Sudāsa.

##### English translation
Indra and Varuna, leaders (of rites), contemplating your affinity, and desirous of cattle, the worshippers, armed with large sickles, have proceeded to the east (to cut the sacred grass): destroy, Indra and Varuna your enemies, whether Dasas or Aryas and defend Sulas with your protection **NOTE**

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यत्रा॒ नर॑ स॒मयं॑ते कृ॒तध्व॑जो॒ यस्मि॑न्ना॒जा भव॑ति॒ किं ॒ ॒
> चन प्रियं ।  
> यत्रा॒ भयं॑ते॒ भुव॑ना स्व॒र्दृश॒स्तत्रा॑ न इंद्रावरु॒णाधि॑  
> वोचतः ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यत्र । नरः । संआयंते । कृतध्वजः । यस्मिन् । आजा । भवति । किं । जन ।  
> प्रियं ।  
> यत्र । भयंते । भुवना । स्वःदृशः । तत्र । नः । इंद्रावरुणा । अधि । वोचतः ॥२॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यत्र यस्मिन्संग्रामे नरो मनुष्याः कृतध्वज उच्छ्रतध्वजाः समयंते
> युद्धार्थं संगच्छंते । यस्मिंश्चाजाजौ युद्दे । चनेति निपातद्वयसमुदायो
> विभज्य योजनीयः । किंच किमपि प्रियमनुकूलं न भवति अपि तु सर्वं दुष्करं
> भवति । यत्र च युद्धे भुवना भुवनानि भूतजातानि स्वर्दृशः शरीरपातादूर्द्ध्वं
> स्वर्गस्य द्रष्टारो वीराश्च भयंते बिभ्यति । तत्र तादृशे संग्रामे हे इंद्रावरुणौ
> नोऽस्मासधि वोचतं । अस्मत्पक्षपातवचनौ भवतं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

In whatever battle the men, with raised banners, assemble for fighting, and in whatever combat nothing whatever is favourable (चन (*cana*) and किं (*kiṃ*) are two particles to be divided and construed separately), but everything becomes hard; and in which battle the beings, and the heroes who see heaven after the body falls, are afraid: in such a battle, O Indra and Varuṇa, speak on our behalf, that is, be speakers who side with us.

### Printed p. 28

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्रावरुणा (*iṃdrāvaruṇā*)—O gods Indra and Varuṇa
- यत्र (*yatra*)—in which battle
- नरः (*naraḥ*)—men
- कृतद्वजः (*kṛtadvajaḥ*)—raising their banners aloft
- समयंते (*samayaṃte*)—assemble for battle
- यस्मिन् (*yasmin*)—in which
- आजा चन (*ājā cana*)—in the very battle
- किंचन (*kiṃcana*)—even a little
- प्रियं (*priyaṃ*)—favourable circumstance
- भवति (*bhavati*)—there is (not)
- यत्र (*yatra*)—in which battle
- भुवना (*bhuvanā*)—all beings
- स्वर्दृशः (*svardṛśaḥ*)—turning their faces towards the sky
- भयंते (*bhayaṃte*)—are afraid
- तत्र (*tatra*)—in such a battle
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- अधि वोचतं (*adhi vocataṃ*)—speak encouraging words

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O gods Indra and Varuṇa, in the battle where men assemble for fighting with banners held aloft, in which there is not even a little favourable circumstance, and in which all beings turn their faces to the sky and are afraid, in such a battle speak encouraging words to us.

##### English translation
Where men assemble with uplifted banners, in whatever conflict, there is something unfavourable; where living beings, looking to heaven, are in fear, there, Indra and Varuna, speak to us (encouragement).

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> सं भूम्या॒ अंता॑ ध्वसि॒रा अ॑दृक्ष॒तेंद्रा॑वरुणा दि॒वि घोष॒  ॑
> आरुहत् ।  
> अस्तु॒र्जना॑ना॒मुप॒ मामरा॑तयो॒ऽर्वागव॑सा हवनश्रु॒ता  ॑
> गतं ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सं । भूम्याः । अंताः । ध्वसिराः । अदृक्षत । इंद्रावरुणा । दिवि । घोषः । आ ।  
> अरुहत् ।

### Printed p. 29

> अस्थुः । जनानां । उप । मां । अरातयः । अर्वाक् । अवसा । हवनश्रुता । आ ।  
> गतं ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्रावरुणौ भूम्या अंताः पर्यंता ध्वसिराः सैनिकैर्ध्वस्ताः समदृक्षत ।
> संदृश्यंते । तथा दिवि द्युलोके घोषः सैनिकानां शब्दश्चारुहत् । अरूढोऽभूत् । जनानामस्मदीयानां भटानामरातयः शत्रवो मामुपास्थुः । उपस्थिताः ।
> एवं प्रवर्तमानेऽस्मिन्युद्धे हे हवनश्रुताह्वानशीलाविंद्रावरुणौ अर्वागस्मदभिमुखमवसा रक्षणेन सहा गतं । अगच्छतु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra and Varuṇa, the ends of the earth are seen laid waste by the soldiers; the clamour of the soldiers has risen to the heaven-world. The enemies of our people, of our warriors, have stood near me. While the battle goes on thus, O Indra and Varuṇa, you who hear the call (हवनश्रुता (*havanaśrutā*) = of calling nature), come towards us with protection.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्रावरुणा (*iṃdrāvaruṇā*)—O Indra and Varuṇa
- भूम्याः (*bhūmyāḥ*)—of the earth
- अंताः (*aṃtāḥ*)—all the ends
- ध्वसिराः (*dhvasirāḥ*)—as if laid waste by soldiers
- समदृक्षत (*samadṛkṣata*)—are seen
- घोषः (*ghoṣaḥ*)—the clamour of soldiers
- दिवि (*divi*)—up to the heaven-world
- आरुहत् (*āruhat*)—has risen
- जनानां (*janānāṃ*)—of our people
- अरातयः (*arātayaḥ*)—all the enemies
- मां (*māṃ*)—me
- उपास्यः (*upāsyaḥ*)—have approached
- हवनश्रुता (*havanaśrutā*)—O Indra and Varuṇa, who hear our entreaty
- अर्वाक् (*arvāk*)—towards us
- अवसा (*avasā*)—with protection
- आ गतं (*ā gataṃ*)—be so kind as to come

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra and Varuṇa, all the ends of the earth appear as if laid waste by soldiers. The clamour of the soldiers has risen up to the heaven-world. All the enemies of our people have approached me. O Indra and Varuṇa, who hear our entreaty, come towards us with protection.

##### English translation
The ends of the earth are beheld laid waste; the clamour has ascended, Indra and Varuna, to heaven: the adversaries of my people approach me : having heard my invocation, come for my defence.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इंद्रा॑वरुणा व॒धना॑भिरप्र॒ति भे॒दं व॒न्वंता॒ प्र सु॒दास॑मावतं ।

### Printed p. 30

> ब्रह्माण्येषां शृणुतं हवीमनि सत्या तृतूनामभवत्पुरोहितिः ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इंद्रावरुणा । वधनाभिः । अप्रति । भेदं । वन्वंता । प्र । सुदासं । अवतं।  
> ब्रह्माणि । एषां । शृणुतं । हवीमनि । सत्या । तृतूनां । अभवत् । पुरःसहितिः ॥४॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्रावरुणेंद्रावरुणौ वधनाभिर्वधकरैरायुध्यॆरप्रतिगतमप्राप्तं भेधमेतत्संज्ञं सुदासः शत्रुं वन्वंता हिंसंतौ युवां सुदासं । शोभनं ददातीति
> सुदाः। एतत्संज्ञं मम याज्यं राजानं प्रावतं। प्रकर्षणारक्षतं । एषां तृत्तूनां
> मम याज्यानां ब्रह्माणि सोत्राणि शृणुतं । अशृणुतं । कदा । हवीमनि । आह्वयंतेऽस्मिन्युद्धार्थं परस्परमिति हवीमा संग्रामः ' तस्मिन् । यस्मादेवं तस्मात्तृतूनामेतत्संज्ञानां मम याज्यानां पुरोहितिमम पुरोधानं सत्या सत्यफलमभवत् । तेषु यन्मम पौरोहित्यं तत्सफलं जातमित्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra and Varuṇa, with the weapons that kill you were slaying the enemy named Bheda, who had not been overcome (अप्रति (*aprati*): unattained, unreached), and you protected Sudāsa fully, my patron the king; Sudāsa is "one who gives well". Hear the praises of these Tṛtsus, my patrons, in the battle; हवीमनि (*havīmani*) is the battle, where they call each other out for fighting. Since it is so, the priestly office I hold for these Tṛtsus has proved true, that is, has borne fruit. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्रावरुणा (*iṃdrāvaruṇā*)—O Indra and Varuṇa
- अप्रति (*aprati*)—not yet defeated
- भेदं (*bhedaṃ*)—Bheda, the enemy of Sudāsa
- वधनाभिः (*vadhanābhiḥ*)—with weapons that serve for killing
- वन्वंता (*vanvaṃtā*)—you who struck
- सुदासं (*sudāsaṃ*)—the king named Sudāsa
- प्र आवतं (*pra āvataṃ*)—protected fully
- हवीमनि (*havīmani*)—in battle
- एषां (*eṣāṃ*)—of these Tṛttus
- ब्रह्माणि (*brahmāṇi*)—the praises
- शृणुतं (*śṛṇutaṃ*)—you heard (therefore)
- तृत्सूनां (*tṛtsūnāṃ*)—for these Tṛttus, the sacrificers
- पुरोहितः (*purohitaḥ*)—my priestly office
- सत्या (*satyā*)—bearing true fruit
- अभवत् (*abhavat*)—became

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra and Varuṇa, you who struck with weapons that serve for killing Bheda, the enemy of Sudāsa, who could not be defeated without your help, protected the king named Sudāsa fully. In battle you heard the praises of the Tṛttu people; therefore my priestly office for these Tṛttus, the sacrificers, became one bearing true fruit.

### Printed p. 31

##### English translation
Indra and Varuna, you protected Sudas, overwhelming the yet unassailed Bheda with your fatal weapons: hear the prayers of these Tritsus in time of battle, so that my ministration may have borne them fruit.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Tṛtsus, Bheda—We have written at length about the Tṛtsus and the king Bheda in Ṛ. Saṃ. Part 21, in the introduction to Sūkta 18 of Maṇḍala 7. Or see what has been written on this subject in the Special Topics of Ṛk 6 of this same sūkta.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इंद्रा॑वरुणाव॒भ्या त॑पंति मा॒घान्य॒र्यो व॒नुषा॒मरा॑तयः।  
> यु॒वं हि वस्व॑ उ॒भय॑स्य॒ राज॒थोऽध॑ स्मा नोऽवतं॒ पार्ये॑  ॒
> दिवि ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इंद्रावरुणौ । अभि । आ । तपंति । मा । अघानि । अर्यः । वनुषां । अरातयः ।  
> युवं । हि । वस्वः । उभयस्य । राजथः । अध । स्म । नः । अवतं । पार्ये । दिवि ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्रावरुणौ आर्योऽरेः शत्रोः संबंधीन्यघान्याहंत्यण्यायुधानि मा
> मामभ्या तपंति । अभितो बाधंते । अपि च वनुषां हिंसकानां मध्येऽरातयोऽभिगमनशीलाः शत्रवश्च मामभितपंति । युवं हि युवां खलूभयस्य पार्थिवस्य
> दिव्यस्य वसो वसुनो धनस्य राजथः । ईशाथे । राजतिरैश्वर्यकर्मा । अध स्मातः
> कारणात् पार्ये तरणीये दिवि दिवसे युद्धदिने नोऽस्मानवतं । रक्षतं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa addresses Indra and Varuṇa. The weapons (अघानि (*aghāni*), "that which strikes") belonging to the enemy अर्यः (*aryaḥ*) (the foe) afflict me on every side. Moreover, among the harmful (वनुषां (*vanuṣāṃ*)) the hostile attackers (अरातयः (*arātayaḥ*), those who come against one) also afflict me. You two are lords (राजथः (*rājathaḥ*), "you rule"; the root राजति (*rājati*) denotes lordship) over the wealth of both kinds, earthly and heavenly. Therefore, on the day of battle, the day that has to be got across, protect us.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्रावरुणौ (*iṃdrāvaruṇau*)—O Indra and Varuṇa
- आर्यः (*āryaḥ*)—belonging to the enemy
- अघानि (*aghāni*)—weapons
- मा (*mā*)—me
- अभ्यातपंति (*abhyātapaṃti*)—afflict completely

### Printed p. 32
- वनुषां (*vanuṣāṃ*)—among the harmful enemies
- अरातयः (*arātayaḥ*)—enemies who fall upon (me) afflict me
- युवं हि (*yuvaṃ hi*)—you, however
- उभयस्य (*ubhayasya*)—of both heaven and earth
- वस्वः (*vasvaḥ*)—of the wealth
- राजथः (*rājathaḥ*)—you are lords
- अध स्म (*adha sma*)—therefore
- पार्ये (*pārye*)—in which hardship is to be crossed
- दिवि (*divi*)—on this day of battle
- नः (*naḥ*)—us
- अवतं (*avataṃ*)—protect

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra and Varuṇa, the enemy's weapons afflict me completely. Those among the harmful enemies who fall upon me afflict me too. You, however, are lords of the wealth of both heaven and earth. On this day of battle, in which hardship has to be crossed, protect us.

##### English translation
Indra and Varuna, the murderous (weapons) of my enemy distress me : foes amongst the malignant (assail me): you two are sovereigns over both (celestial and terrestrial) wealth : protect us therefore on the day of battle.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Divi—on the day, that is, on the day when the battle is going on. The battle here is the one that took place between Sudāsa and his enemies, the ten kings beginning with Śimyu. For the description of this battle, see what has been written in the Special Topics of Ṛk 6 of this same sūkta.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यु॒वां ह॑वंत उ॒भया॑स आ॒जिष्विंद्रं॑ च॒ वस्वो॒ वरु॑णं च  ॒॑
> सातये ।  
> यत्र॒ राज॑भिर्द॒शभि॒र्निबा॑धितं॒ प्र सु॒दास॒माव॑तं॒ तृत्सु॑भिः स॒ह॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> युवां । हवंते । उभयासः । अजिषु । इंद्रं । च । वस्वः । वरुणं । च ।  
> सातये ।  
> यत्र । राजभिः । दशभिः । निबाधितं । प्र । सुदासं । आवतं । तृत्सुभिः । सह ॥

### Printed p. 33

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> उभयास उभयविधाः सुदाः संज्ञो राजा तत्सहायभूतास्तत्सवश्चैवं
> द्विप्रकारा जना आजिषु संग्रामेष्ट्विंद्रं च वरुणं च युवां हवंते । आह्वयंते ।
> किमर्थं । वस्वो धनस्य सातये संभजनार्थं । यत्र येष्वाजिषु दशभिर्दशसंख्याकै
> राजभिः शत्रुभूतैनृपैन्रिबाधितं नितरां हिंसितं सुदासं तृप्सुभिः सह वर्तमानं
> प्रावतं युवां प्रकर्षणारक्षतं । तेष्णाजिष्टित्यन्वयः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

उभयासः (*ubhayāsaḥ*) means "of two kinds": the king named Sudās, and his helpers the Tṛtsus. People of these two kinds call upon (हवंते (*havaṃte*), "invite") you, Indra and Varuṇa, in battles. For what purpose? For the sharing (सातये (*sātaye*)) of wealth. The construction is "in those battles in which you protected Sudās, who was hard pressed by ten kings, hostile kings, together with the Tṛtsus, you protected him excellently" (प्रावतं (*prāvataṃ*), "protect forth").

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O Indra and Varuṇa) यत्र (*yatra*)—in which battles
- दशभिः (*daśabhiḥ*)—by ten
- राजभिः (*rājabhiḥ*)—by enemy kings
- निबाधितं (*nibādhitaṃ*)—completely harassed
- सुदासं (*sudāsaṃ*)—the king Sudāsa
- तृत्सुभिः सह (*tṛtsubhiḥ saha*)—together with the Tṛtsus
- प्र आवतं (*pra āvataṃ*)—you protected completely, in such
- आजिषु (*ājiṣu*)—battles
- उभयासः (*ubhayāsaḥ*)—all on the side of Sudāsa and of the Tṛtsus
- इंद्रं च (*iṃdraṃ ca*)—Indra by name
- वरुणं च (*varuṇaṃ ca*)—and Varuṇa by name
- युवां (*yuvāṃ*)—you two alone
- वस्वः (*vasvaḥ*)—of wealth
- सातये (*sātaye*)—for the gain
- हवंते (*havaṃte*)—invite with prayer

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra and Varuṇa, in those battles in which you protected completely, together with the Tṛtsus, the king Sudāsa who was completely harassed by ten enemy kings, in such battles all on the side of Sudāsa and the Tṛtsus invite you two, Indra and Varuṇa, with prayer, for the gain of wealth.

##### English translation
Both (Sudas and the Tritsus) call upon you two, (Indra and Varuna), in combats for the acquirement of wealth, when you defend Sudas, together with the Tritsus, when attacked by the ten Rajas.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In this Ṛk and in the next two Ṛks there is mention of the battle between the king Sudāsa and his enemies, the ten kings. This matter is also stated in Sūktas 18 and 33 of this same maṇḍala. It is necessary to give a detailed account of it here.

At one time the king Sudāsa had to fight with ten kings who were his opponents: Śimyu, Turvaśa, Druhyu, Kavaṣa, Pūru, Anu, Bheda, Śambara, and two kings named Vaikarṇa, ten kings in all.

### Printed p. 34
This battle seems to have been very fierce and terrible. Sūkta 18 of this same maṇḍala describes how a people called the Tṛtsus helped Sudāsa in this battle, and how, as a result of this help, Sudāsa defeated the ten kings mentioned above. Besides, this battle is mentioned in Sūkta 33 of this same maṇḍala and in the present sūkta (83). It is understood that this battle took place on the bank of the river Paruṣṇī (in the Punjab). On Sudāsa's side the king Yadu is also said to have helped, bringing his followers, the peoples called the Matsyas, Sakthas, Bhalānas, Alinas, Viṣāṇins, Śivas, Ajas, Yakṣus and Śigrus. Another such battle is understood to have taken place on the bank of the river Yamunā with an enemy king called Bheda. This Bheda is understood to have been the leader of the Dasyu people, the earlier inhabitants of this country. The seer of this maṇḍala, Vasiṣṭha, must have had a special relationship with the king of the Tṛtsu people, and chiefly with Sudāsa. Vasiṣṭha may have been the priest of the king of the Tṛtsus. It is understood that Viśvāmitra had earlier been the priest of Sudāsa, king of the Tṛtsus, and that Viśvāmitra was with him when this king was fighting enemy kings in the river regions of the Vipāś (Beas) and the Śutudrī (Sutlej). But in the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa (Ai. Brā. 7-34-9) it is said that Sudāsa was a very famous king and that Vasiṣṭha was his priest. Since it is customary to call Sudāsa Paijavana, it may be said that he was the son or descendant of Pijavana, and the grandson of Divodāsa.

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)
